  • Getting the leak taken down. Her last live request - 'ive got a leak. someone using my stuff on a swingers website x' on Aug 31, URL supplied at 03:59 on Sep 1, 11 days of silence since.   • Seeing any output at all - a posting report, a growth number, an OF update. In 79 days this chat has carried nothing but requests, invoices and reel links.
  • Fast, hand-held support. New to Telegram, does not know platform mechanics ('yeah how do i do it'); the one thing she ever praised twice was the 7-minute Aug 21 fix.
  • Not being treated as a debtor. She paid five cycles without complaint; the only two messages she has received since resigning are payment chases, one threatening to pause work that does not exist.
  • Not being made to feel like the problem. She apologised in nearly every message, absorbed four lockouts, seven credential requests and a 30-reel monthly quota without complaint, and even her resignation opens with thanks and an apology.

Wins to highlight back

  • Mayson responded to the resignation in 72 minutes with exactly the right move - 'Hey Paige would you like to hop on a call today and go over it?'. It remains the fastest and most appropriate reaction anyone has had on this account in eleven weeks. That it came from a chatter rather than leadership, and that nobody built on it in the nine days since, is the story of the account in one line.
  • Michelle broke a 44-day silence within seven hours of the leak report and claimed it: 'send me the link of this babes and we will take care of it'. The follow-through never came, but the reflex was right and for a few hours the client felt heard.
  • Stephski closed the only carry-forward item resolved anywhere in this window, acknowledging the Aug 26 payment on Aug 29 with 'Amazing thank you!'. Invoicing remains the only function on this account that consistently follows up on itself - which is also precisely the problem, because it is now the only function still talking to her.
  • Paige has still not disputed anything, gone public, or gone hostile. She thanked the team on the way out, apologised for leaving, paid five of six cycles without complaint, and gave notice rather than vanishing.   • Instagram reach and trial-account performance as the top of her OnlyFans funnel — raised Aug 31 after her reels fell from 200k+ to under 3k, which she attributes to the 'not shown to below 18' flag despite being told otherwise at owner level; she ran the prescribed hook test herself within two days

  • Control over which of her accounts ALTR gets access to — she distinguishes her personal Snapchat from her OF one ('I have my personal and an of one', Sep 10) and has previously worried about fans migrating off OnlyFans ('Only fear is they’ll start talking to me there more and not of', Jul 31)
